\xi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\setminus\{0\}
and
\varepsilon>0

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\lVert{\mathfrak{D}_{\varepsilon,\xi^{\prime}}({z})Q_{\eta,\tau}q-\mathfrak{D}_{0,\xi^{\prime}}({z})Q_{\eta,\tau}q}\rVert}_{L^{2}((-1,1);\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}((-1,1);\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}{\lVert{q}\rVert}_{L^{\infty}((-1,1))}(\lvert{\eta}\rvert+\lvert{\tau}\rvert)\varepsilon(1+|\xi^{\prime}|)=\varepsilon\frac{\delta_{1}^{-1/2}(1+|\xi^{\prime}|)}{C_{3}}.

Moreover, Lemma
4.13
(with
\mathfrak{M}(\xi^{\prime})=I+\mathfrak{D}_{0,\xi^{\prime}}({z})Q_{\eta,\tau}q
for
\xi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
) and (
4.40
) imply

\displaystyle\textup{ess sup}_{\xi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}}

\displaystyle\|(I+\mathfrak{D}_{0,\xi^{\prime}}({z})Q_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}\|_{L^{2}((-1,1);\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}((-1,1);\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|(I+D^{{0,\kappa}}_{0}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}\|_{0\to 0}\leq\frac{C_{3}}{2}.

Hence, as
1+R=\delta_{1}^{-1/2}
, we can estimate similarly as in the first part of the proof for
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{1})

\displaystyle\textup{ess sup}_{\lvert{\xi^{\prime}}\rvert\leq R}{\lVert{(I+\mathfrak{D}_{\varepsilon,\xi^{\prime}}({z})Q_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}}\rVert}_{L^{2}((-1,1);\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}((-1,1);\mathbb{C}^{N})}

(4.43)

\displaystyle=\textup{ess sup}_{\lvert{\xi^{\prime}}\rvert\leq R}\big\|\big[I+(I+\mathfrak{D}_{0,\xi^{\prime}}({z})Q_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}\bigl(\mathfrak{D}_{\varepsilon,\xi^{\prime}}({z})Q_{\eta,\tau}q-\mathfrak{D}_{0,\xi^{\prime}}({z})Q_{\eta,\tau}q\bigr)\big]^{-1}

\displaystyle\cdot(I+\mathfrak{D}_{0,\xi^{\prime}}({z})Q_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}\big\|_{L^{2}((-1,1);\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}((-1,1);\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\frac{C_{3}}{2}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon\delta_{1}^{-1/2}(1+R)}{C_{3}}}\cdot\frac{C_{3}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\frac{C_{3}}{2}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon\delta_{1}^{-1}}{C_{3}}}\cdot\frac{C_{3}}{2}\leq C_{3}.

Combining (
4.42
) and (
4.43
), and applying Lemma
4.13
gives us

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\lVert{(I+D_{\varepsilon}^{0,\kappa}({z})Q_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}}\rVert}_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle=\max\big\{\textup{ess sup}_{|\xi^{\prime}|\geq R}{\lVert{(I+\mathfrak{D}_{\varepsilon,\xi^{\prime}}({z})Q_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}}\rVert}_{L^{2}((-1,1);\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}((-1,1);\mathbb{C}^{N})},

\displaystyle\textup{ess sup}_{|\xi^{\prime}|<R}{\lVert{(I+\mathfrak{D}_{\varepsilon,\xi^{\prime}}({z})Q_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}}\rVert}_{L^{2}((-1,1);\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}((-1,1);\mathbb{C}^{N})}\big\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}

for
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{1})
.
∎

Corollary 4.18
.

Let
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
,
q
be as in (
1.4
),
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
be compact and
\max_{(\eta,\tau)\in S}\eta^{2}-\tau^{2}<\tfrac{\pi^{2}}{4}
. Then, there exists a
\delta_{2}=\delta_{2}(S)>0
such that

\sup_{(\varepsilon,y_{0},(\eta,\tau),\kappa)\in(0,\delta_{2})\times\mathbb{R}\times S\times\textup{SO}(\theta)}{\lVert{(I+D^{{y_{0},\kappa}}_{\varepsilon}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}}\rVert}_{0\to 0}<\infty.

Proof.

Since
D_{\varepsilon}^{y_{0},\kappa}(z)=D_{\varepsilon}^{0,\kappa}(z)
, see the text below (
4.31
), the assertion follows directly from Proposition
4.17
if we can show

\sup_{((\eta,\tau),\kappa)\in S\times\textup{SO}(\theta)}C_{3}(\eta,\tau,\kappa)<\infty\quad\textup{and}\quad\inf_{((\eta,\tau),\kappa)\in S\times\textup{SO}(\theta)}\delta_{1}(\eta,\tau,\kappa)>0,

with
C_{3}
and
\delta_{1}
as in Proposition
4.17
. Note also that as
S
is bounded the first inequality and (
4.41
) imply the second inequality in the above displayed formula. Moreover, the assumption
\max_{(\eta,\tau)\in S}\eta^{2}-\tau^{2}<\tfrac{\pi^{2}}{4}
implies
\max_{(\eta,\tau)\in S}C_{2}(\eta,\tau)<\infty
, where
C_{2}
was defined in (
4.39
). Hence, it follows from (
4.40
) that the inequality
\sup_{((\eta,\tau),\kappa)\in S\times\textup{SO}(\theta)}C_{3}(\eta,\tau,\kappa)<\infty
is fulfilled if

\sup_{((\eta,\tau),\kappa)\in S\times\textup{SO}(\theta)}\|(I+D^{{0,\kappa}}_{0}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}\|_{0\to 0}<\infty.

(4.44)

Obviously, the operator
D^{{0,\kappa}}_{0}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}q
depends continuously on
\eta
,
\tau
with respect to the operator norm in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
. According to Proposition
4.11

D^{{0,\kappa}}_{0}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}q
depends also continuously on
\kappa
with respect to the operator norm. Moreover, Proposition
4.17
gives us that the operator
I+D^{{0,\kappa}}_{0}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}q
is continuously invertible in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
for
((\eta,\tau),\kappa)\in S\times\textup{SO}(\theta)
. Thus, as
S\times\textup{SO}(\theta)
is compact, (
4.44
) is indeed true.
∎

4.4.2.
\Sigma
is a rotated
C^{2}_{b}
-graph

After treating the case of affine hyperplanes, we turn to the case where
\Sigma
is a rotated
C^{2}_{b}
-graph as in (
4.1
), i.e. there exist
{\zeta\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})}
and
\kappa\in\textup{SO}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta})
such that

\Sigma=\Sigma_{\zeta,\kappa}=\{\kappa(x^{\prime},\zeta(x^{\prime})):x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\}.

Moreover, recall that
x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})=\kappa(x^{\prime},\zeta(x^{\prime}))
and
\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})=\nu(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}))
,
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
, see (
4.25
). According to
[
8
, Proposition A.2 (i), first line of the proof of Proposition 2.4, and eq. (3.11)]
there exists a
C_{4}=C_{4}(\Sigma)>0
such that for all
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}\in(-2\varepsilon_{2},2\varepsilon_{2})

C_{4}^{-1}\big(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|\big)\leq|x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-x_{\zeta,\kappa}(y^{\prime})+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})|\leq C_{4}\big(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|\big),

(4.45)

with
\varepsilon_{2}>0
chosen as in Proposition
4.6
.
We are going to prove the uniform boundedness of
(I+B_{\varepsilon}(z)Vq)^{-1}
in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\Sigma)
with respect to
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{3})
, for a suitable
\varepsilon_{3}\in(0,\varepsilon_{2})
. It follows from (
4.19
), (
4.21
), (
4.22
), and (
4.29
) that this is equivalent to proving the uniform boundedness of
(I+D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}=\iota_{\zeta,\kappa}(I+\overline{B}_{\varepsilon}(z)Vq)^{-1}\iota_{\zeta,\kappa}^{-1}
.

We start by analysing
D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)
locally. To do so we need to introduce further notations. For
x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
we define

\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}):=\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime})+\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-x_{0}^{\prime}\rangle,\qquad x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}.

(4.46)

Moreover, we define the localisation parameter
a_{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon^{1/6}
for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{2})
.
Next, we introduce a family of auxiliary operators. For this, we choose a
C^{\infty}
-function
\omega
with
0\leq\omega\leq 1
,
\omega=1
on
\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\setminus B(0,1)
and
\omega=0
on
B(0,\frac{1}{2})
. We use this function to cut out the singular part of the integral kernel of
D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)
; cf. (
4.26
). More precisely, in analogy with (
4.27
) and (
4.28
), we define for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{2})
and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}\in(-2\varepsilon_{2},2\varepsilon_{2})\setminus\{0\}
the operators

\displaystyle e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)

\displaystyle:L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N}),

(4.47)

\displaystyle e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)g(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}}G_{z}(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-x_{\zeta,\kappa}(y^{\prime})+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}))\omega\bigl(\tfrac{x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\cdot\sqrt{1+\lvert{\nabla\zeta(y^{\prime})}\rvert^{2}}g(y^{\prime})\,dy^{\prime},

and

E_{{\varepsilon}}(z):\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})\to\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}),\qquad E_{{\varepsilon}}(z)f(t):=\int_{-1}^{1}e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\varepsilon(t-s)}(z)f(s)\,ds.

(4.48)

We start by proving preliminary results for
e_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(z)
and
d^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)
. Afterwards, we transfer these results to
E_{\varepsilon}(z)
and
D^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\varepsilon}(z)
in Proposition
4.24
.
For the estimates regarding
e_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(z)
and
d^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)
the following lemma turns out to be useful.

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
z\in\rho(H)
and
G_{z}
be defined by (
4.4
)–(
4.5
). Then, there exist
C_{5}=C_{5}(z,m)>0
and
C_{6}=C_{6}(z,m)>0
such that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta}\setminus\{0\}
and
j\in\{1,\dots,\theta\}

\displaystyle|G_{z}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}|x|^{1-\theta}e^{-C_{6}|x|},

(4.49)

\displaystyle|\partial_{j}G_{z}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}|x|^{-\theta}e^{-C_{6}|x|}.

In particular, there holds for all
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
,
j\in\{1,\dots,\theta\}
and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}\in(-2\varepsilon_{2},2\varepsilon_{2})

\displaystyle|G_{z}(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-x_{\zeta,\kappa}(y^{\prime})+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}))|

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}C_{4}^{\theta-1}(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{1-\theta}e^{-\frac{C_{6}}{C_{4}}|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|},

(4.50)

\displaystyle|\partial_{j}G_{z}(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-x_{\zeta,\kappa}(y^{\prime})+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}))|

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}C_{4}^{\theta}(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{-\theta}e^{-\frac{C_{6}}{C_{4}}|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|}.

Proof.

Rough estimations and asymptotic expansions of the modified Bessel functions
K_{1}
and
K_{0}
, see
[
27
, §10.25 (ii) and §10.30 (i)]
and
[
8
, eq. (B.8)]
, lead to

|G_{z}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|^{1-\theta})e^{-\textup{Im}\sqrt{z^{2}-m^{2}}|x|}

and

|\partial_{j}G_{z}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|^{-\theta})e^{-\textup{Im}\sqrt{z^{2}-m^{2}}|x|}

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta}\setminus\{0\}
and
j\in\{1,\dots,\theta\}
, where
C=C(m,z)>0
is a constant which only depends on
m
and
z
. Thus, (
4.49
) is valid if one chooses
C_{6}\in(0,\textup{Im}\sqrt{z^{2}-m^{2}})
and

C_{5}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta}\setminus\{0\},l\in\{\theta-1,\theta\}}C\frac{1+|x|^{-l}}{|x|^{-l}}e^{-(\textup{Im }\sqrt{m^{2}-z^{2}}-C_{6})|x|}<\infty.

Furthermore, combining these estimates with (
4.45
) implies (
4.50
).
∎

Lemma 4.20
.

Let
z\in\rho(H)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{2})
,
a_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{1/6}
, and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}\in(-2\varepsilon_{2},2\varepsilon_{2})\setminus\{0\}
. Then, the operator
e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)
acts as a bounded operator from
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
to
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
and

{\lVert{e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)}\rVert}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\leq C\frac{1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert}{a_{\varepsilon}},

where
C>0
does not depend on
\widetilde{\varepsilon}
and
\varepsilon
. Moreover, for
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
the mapping
(-2\varepsilon_{2},2\varepsilon_{2})\setminus\{0\}\ni\widetilde{\varepsilon}\mapsto e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
is continuous.

Proof.

We aim to prove the assertion by applying Lemma
A.1
. To do so, it is necessary to find suitable estimates for the integral kernel of
e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)
which is for
x^{\prime},y^{\prime} \in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
given by

k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}):=G_{z}(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-x_{\zeta,\kappa}(y^{\prime})+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}))\omega\bigl(\tfrac{x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)\sqrt{1+\lvert{\nabla\zeta(y^{\prime})}\rvert^{2}}.

We notice as
G_{z}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta}\setminus\{0\};\mathbb{C}^{N\times N})
,
\zeta\in C_{b}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
, and
\omega\in C_{b}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
and as
\omega
cuts out the singularity of
G_{z}
, we have
k\in C_{b}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\times\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N\times N})
. Furthermore, using (
4.50
),
0\leq\omega\leq 1
,
\textup{supp }\omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\setminus B(0,\frac{1}{2})
and
\zeta\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
immediately gives us for
x^{\prime}\neq y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}

\displaystyle|k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq C\chi_{\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\setminus B(0,1/2)}\bigl(\tfrac{x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{1-\theta}e^{-c|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|}

\displaystyle\leq C\chi_{\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\setminus B(0,1/2)}\bigl(\tfrac{x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|^{1-\theta}e^{-c|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|},

where
c=\tfrac{C_{6}}{C_{4}}
with
C_{4}>0
from (
4.45
) and
C_{6}
from Lemma
4.19
. Next, we estimate the derivatives with respect to
x^{\prime}
of
k
. The derivative with respect to
x_{l}^{\prime}
,
l\in\{1,\dots\theta-1\}
, is given for for
x^{\prime}\neq y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
by

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx^{\prime}_{l}}k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\theta}\Big((\partial_{j}G_{z})(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-x_{\zeta,\kappa}(y^{\prime})+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{d}{dx^{\prime}_{l}}(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})[j]+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})[j])\omega\bigl(\tfrac{x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)\sqrt{1+\lvert{\nabla\zeta(y^{\prime})}\rvert^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle+G_{z}(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-x_{\zeta,\kappa}(y^{\prime})+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}))\frac{1}{a_{\varepsilon}}(\partial_{l}\omega)\bigl(\tfrac{x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)\sqrt{1+\lvert{\nabla\zeta(y^{\prime})}\rvert^{2}},

where
v[j]
denotes the
j
-th component of a vector
v
.
Applying (
4.50
), the properties of
\omega
and
\zeta
again, we can estimate for
x^{\prime}\neq y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}

\displaystyle\Bigl|\frac{d}{dx^{\prime}_{l}}k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C\chi_{\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\setminus B(0,1/2)}\bigl(\tfrac{x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)\Bigl((|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{-\theta}e^{-c|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{a_{\varepsilon}}(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{1-\theta}e^{-c|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C\chi_{\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\setminus B(0,1/2)}\bigl(\tfrac{x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)\Bigl(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|^{-\theta}+\frac{1}{a_{\varepsilon}}|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|^{1-\theta}\Bigr)e^{-c|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|}.

Thus, if we set
\widetilde{k}(z^{\prime}):=C\chi_{\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\setminus B(0,1/2)}\bigl(\tfrac{z^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)\Bigl(|z^{\prime}|^{-\theta}+\tfrac{1}{a_{\varepsilon}}|z^{\prime}|^{1-\theta}\Bigr)e^{-c|z^{\prime}|}
for
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\setminus\{0\}
we get

|k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})|,\,\sum_{l=1}^{\theta-1}\Bigl|\frac{d}{dx^{\prime}_{l}}k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\Bigr|\leq\widetilde{k}(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}),\quad x^{\prime}\neq y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}.

(4.51)

Hence, by Lemma
A.1
the map
e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)
acts as a bounded operator from
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
to
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
and

{\lVert{e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)}\rVert}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\leq C\|\widetilde{k}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}.

Now, the norm estimate in the assertion follows from

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{k}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}

\displaystyle=C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}}\chi_{\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\setminus B(0,1/2)}\bigl(\tfrac{z^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)\Big(|z^{\prime}|^{-\theta}+\frac{1}{a_{\varepsilon}}|z^{\prime}|^{1-\theta}\Big)e^{-c|z^{\prime}|}dz^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{a_{\varepsilon}/2}^{\infty}\Big(r^{-\theta}+\frac{1}{a_{\varepsilon}}r^{1-\theta}\Big)e^{-cr}r^{\theta-2}\,dr

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\frac{1}{a_{\varepsilon}}+\frac{1+\lvert{\log(a_{\varepsilon})}\rvert}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigg)\leq C\frac{1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert}{a_{\varepsilon}}.

Finally, we prove the continuity. To do so, let
\widetilde{\varepsilon}\in(-2\varepsilon_{2},2\varepsilon_{2})\setminus\{0\}
and
(\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence such that
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n}\in(-2\varepsilon_{2},2\varepsilon_{2})\setminus\{0\}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n}\overset{n\to\infty}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{\varepsilon}
. Using the dominated convergence theorem and (
4.51
) shows that for
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})

e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n}}f
and
\partial_{l}e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n}}f
,
l\in\{1,\dots,\theta-1\}
, converge pointwise to
e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}f
and
\partial_{l}e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}f
,
{l\in\{1,\dots,\theta-1\}}
, respectively. Furthermore, (
4.51
) shows that
|e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n}}f|
and
|\partial_{l}e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n}}f|
,
l\in\{1,\dots,\theta-1\}
, are independently of
n\in\mathbb{N}
pointwise bounded by the function
|f|*\widetilde{k}
which is by Young’s inequality square integrable as
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
and
\widetilde{k}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
. Hence, applying the dominated converge theorem again shows that
e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n}}f
converges to
e^{a_{\varepsilon}}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}f
in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
.
∎

Lemma 4.21
.

Let
z\in\rho(H)
,
\psi\in C^{1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
,
x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
,
\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}
be as in (
4.46
), and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}\in(-2\varepsilon_{2},2\varepsilon_{2})\setminus\{0\}
. Then,

\big\|[d^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z),\psi]\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\leq C{\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}(1+\lvert{\log\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert}\rvert),

where
C>0
does not depend on
\widetilde{\varepsilon}\neq 0
and
x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
. Moreover, for
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
the mapping
(-2\varepsilon_{2},2\varepsilon_{2})\setminus\{0\}\ni\widetilde{\varepsilon}\mapsto[d^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z),\psi]f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
is continuous.

Proof.

We prove this result in the same vein as the previous lemma, i.e. we estimate the integral kernel of
[d^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z),\psi]
and its partial derivatives, and apply Lemma
A.1
. The integral kernel of
[d^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z),\psi]
is given for
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
by

k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}):=G_{z}(x_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(x^{\prime})-x_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(y^{\prime})+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(x^{\prime}))\sqrt{1+\lvert{\nabla\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(y^{\prime})}\rvert^{2}}(\psi(y^{\prime})-\psi(x^{\prime})).

Using

\displaystyle x_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\kappa(x^{\prime},\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime})+\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-x_{0}^{\prime}\rangle),

(4.52)

\displaystyle\nu_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa(-\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),1)}{\sqrt{1+|\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime})|^{2}}}=\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\nabla\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime})

for
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
shows that
k
can be simplified to

k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})=G_{z}\bigl(\kappa(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime},\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle)+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}_{0})\bigr)\sqrt{1+\lvert{\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime})}\rvert^{2}}(\psi(y^{\prime})-\psi(x^{\prime})).

Moreover, with (
4.52
),
\kappa\in\textup{SO}(\theta)
, and the Pythagorean theorem one gets

\displaystyle|\kappa(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime},\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle)

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}_{0})|^{2}

\displaystyle=|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|^{2}+\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle^{2}+\widetilde{\varepsilon}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|^{2}(1+\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}^{2})+\widetilde{\varepsilon}^{2}.

In particular, we can choose
C_{4}^{\prime}>0
which does not depend on
x_{0}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}
such that

\displaystyle(C_{4}^{\prime})^{-1}(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)

\displaystyle\leq|\kappa(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime},\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle)+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}_{0})|

(4.53)

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}^{\prime}(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|).

Then, (
4.49
), (
4.53
), the Lipschitz continuity of
\psi\in C^{1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
and
\zeta\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
yield

\displaystyle|k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq C(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{1-\theta}e^{-c^{\prime}|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|}{\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}|x^{\prime}-y’|

\displaystyle\leq C{\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{2-\theta}e^{-c^{\prime}|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|},\quad x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1},

where
c^{\prime}=\tfrac{C_{6}}{C_{4}^{\prime}}
with
C_{6}
from Lemma
4.19
and
C>0
is independent of
x_{0}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}
.
The derivative with respect to
x_{l}^{\prime}
,
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
, of
k
is given by

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx^{\prime}_{l}}k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{\theta}(\partial_{j}G_{z})\bigl(\kappa(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime},\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle)+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}_{0})\bigr)

\displaystyle\cdot\bigl(\kappa(e_{l}^{\prime},\partial_{l}\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}))\bigr)[j](\psi(y^{\prime})-\psi(x^{\prime}))

\displaystyle-G_{z}\bigl(\kappa(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime},\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle)+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}_{0})\bigr)(\partial_{l}\psi)(x^{\prime})\biggr)

\displaystyle\cdot\sqrt{1+\lvert{\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime})}\rvert^{2}},

where
e_{l}^{\prime}
denotes the
l
-th Euclidean unit vector in
\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
and
\bigl(\kappa(e_{l}^{\prime},\partial_{l}\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}))\bigr)[j]
denotes the
j
-th entry of the vector
\kappa(e_{l}^{\prime},\partial_{l}\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}))
.
Using (
4.49
), (
4.53
), the Lipschitz continuity of
\psi\in C^{1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
and
\zeta\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
again gives us

\displaystyle\Bigl|\frac{d}{dx^{\prime}_{l}}k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C{\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}\Big((|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{-\theta}e^{-c^{\prime}|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|}|x^{\prime}-y’|

\displaystyle+(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{1-\theta}e^{-c^{\prime}|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C{\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{1-\theta}e^{-c^{\prime}|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|}

for all
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
, where
C>0
can again be chosen independently of
x_{0}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}
.
Setting for
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}

\widetilde{k}(z^{\prime}):=C{\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}\big((|z^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{2-\theta}+(|z^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{1-\theta}\big)e^{-c^{\prime}|z^{\prime}|}

leads to

|k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})|,\,\sum_{l=1}^{\theta-1}\Bigl|\frac{d}{dx^{\prime}_{l}}k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\Bigr|\leq\widetilde{k}(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}),\quad x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}.

Now, Lemma
A.1
shows

\displaystyle\big\|[d^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)

\displaystyle,\psi]\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}}\widetilde{k}(z^{\prime})\,dz^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C{\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big((r+\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert)^{2-\theta}+(r+\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert)^{1-\theta}\big)e^{-c^{\prime}r}r^{\theta-2}\,dr

\displaystyle\leq C{\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(1+(r+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{-1}\big)e^{-c^{\prime}r}\,dr

\displaystyle\leq C{\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}(1+\lvert{\log\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert}\rvert).

The assertion regarding the continuity can be proven in a similar way as in Lemma
4.20
.
∎

Lemma 4.22
.

Let
z\in\rho(H)
,
x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
,
\zeta\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
be as described at the beginning of Section
4.4.2
,
\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}
be as in (
4.46
),
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{2})
,
a_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{1/6}
, and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}\in(-2\varepsilon_{2},2\varepsilon_{2})\setminus\{0\}
. Then, there exists a
\delta_{3}=\delta_{3}(\zeta)\in(0,\varepsilon_{2})
such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{3})
the inequality

\big\|\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big(d^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)-d^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)\big)\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\leq Ca_{\varepsilon}(1+\lvert{\log\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert}\rvert)

holds,
where
C>0
does not depend on
\varepsilon
,
\widetilde{\varepsilon}
, and
x_{0}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We prove this statement by estimating the integral kernel of the operator

\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},a_{\varepsilon})}\big(d^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)-d^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)\big)\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},a_{\varepsilon})}

and applying Lemma
A.1
. The mentioned integral kernel is given for
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
by

\displaystyle k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}):=

\displaystyle\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}(x^{\prime})\bigg(G_{z}(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-x_{\zeta,\kappa}(y^{\prime})+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}))\sqrt{1+\lvert{\nabla\zeta(y^{\prime})}\rvert^{2}}

\displaystyle-G_{z}(\kappa(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime},\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle)+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime}))\sqrt{1+\lvert{\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime})}\rvert^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\cdot\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}(y^{\prime}).

If
x^{\prime}\not\in B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})
or
y^{\prime}\not\in B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})
, then
k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})=0
. Thus, we assume from now on
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})
.
Using the mean value theorem for matrix-valued functions, cf.
[
8
, Lemma A.1]
, (
4.49
), and
\zeta\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
we find

\displaystyle|k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\theta}\sup_{\upsilon\in[0,1],j\in\{1,\dots\theta\}}|\partial_{j}G_{z}(w_{\upsilon})\sqrt{1+|\nabla\zeta(y^{\prime})|^{2}}||w_{1}-w_{0}|

(4.54)

\displaystyle+\Bigl|G_{z}(w_{0})\bigl(\sqrt{1+|\nabla\zeta(y^{\prime})|^{2}}-\sqrt{1+|\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime})|^{2}}\bigr)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\sup_{\upsilon\in[0,1]}|w_{\upsilon}|^{-\theta}|w_{1}-w_{0}|+|w_{0}|^{1-\theta}|x_{0}^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|\Big)

with

w_{\upsilon}=\upsilon\big(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-x_{\zeta,\kappa}(y^{\prime})+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})\big)+(1-\upsilon)\big(\kappa(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime},\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle)+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})\big)

for
\upsilon\in[0,1]
. Note that the mean value theorem is applicable since
w_{\upsilon}\neq 0
for
\upsilon\in[0,1]
, see (
4.56
) below.
Next, we want to estimate

\displaystyle|w_{1}-

\displaystyle w_{0}|

\displaystyle=|x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-x_{\zeta,\kappa}(y^{\prime})+\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-\kappa(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime},\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle)-\widetilde{\varepsilon}\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=|\kappa(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime},\zeta(x^{\prime})-\zeta(y^{\prime}))

\displaystyle-\kappa(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime},\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle)+\widetilde{\varepsilon}(\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime}))|

\displaystyle=|\kappa(0,\zeta(x^{\prime})-\zeta(y^{\prime})-\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle)+\widetilde{\varepsilon}(\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime}))|.

As
\zeta\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
and
\kappa\in\textup{SO}(\theta)
, there exists a
K=K(\zeta)>0
such that

\lvert{\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})}\rvert=\bigg|\frac{\kappa(-\nabla\zeta(x^{\prime}),1)}{\sqrt{1+|\nabla\zeta(x^{\prime})|^{2}}}-\frac{\kappa(-\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),1)}{\sqrt{1+|\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime})|^{2}}}\bigg|\leq K\lvert{x^{\prime}-x_{0}^{\prime}}\rvert\leq 3Ka_{\varepsilon}

and

\displaystyle|\kappa(0,\zeta(x^{\prime})-\zeta(y^{\prime})-\langle

\displaystyle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle)|

\displaystyle=|\zeta(x^{\prime})-\zeta(y^{\prime})-\langle\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\int_{0}^{1}\langle\nabla\zeta(y^{\prime}+t(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}))-\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime}),x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle\,dt\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq K\bigg|\int_{0}^{1}|t(x^{\prime}-x_{0}^{\prime})+(1-t)(y^{\prime}-x_{0}^{\prime})||x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|\,dt\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 3Ka_{\varepsilon}|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|,

where we used
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})
. Hence, if
\delta_{3}=\delta_{3}(\zeta)\in(0,\varepsilon_{2})
is chosen sufficiently small, then for all
a_{\varepsilon}\in(0,\delta_{3}^{1/6})
the inequality

|w_{1}-w_{0}|\leq K3a_{\varepsilon}(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)\leq\frac{1}{2C_{4}}(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)

(4.55)

holds with
C_{4}>0
from (
4.45
).
Therefore, we can use (
4.45
) to estimate
|w_{\upsilon}|
,
\upsilon\in[0,1]
, from below by

\displaystyle|w_{\upsilon}|

\displaystyle=|\upsilon w_{1}+(1-\upsilon)w_{0}|=|w_{1}+(1-\upsilon)(w_{0}-w_{1})|\geq|w_{1}|-|w_{1}-w_{0}|

(4.56)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{C_{4}}(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)-\frac{1}{2C_{4}}(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)=\frac{1}{2C_{4}}(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|).

Thus, plugging (
4.55
) and (
4.56
) into (
4.54
) yields for
a_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{1/6}
with
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{3})

\lvert{k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})}\rvert\leq\begin{cases}0&\textup{if }x^{\prime}\not\in B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})\textup{ or }y^{\prime}\not\in B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon}),\\
Ca_{\varepsilon}(\lvert{x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}}\rvert+\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert)^{1-\theta}&\textup{else}.\end{cases}

Applying Lemma
A.1
yields

\displaystyle\big\|\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big(d^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)-d^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)\big)

\displaystyle\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leq Ca_{\varepsilon}\int_{B(0,6a_{\varepsilon})}(\lvert{z^{\prime}}\rvert+\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert)^{1-\theta}\,dz^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq Ca_{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{6a_{\varepsilon}}(r+\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert)^{1-\theta}r^{\theta-2}\,dr

\displaystyle\leq Ca_{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{6a_{\varepsilon}}(r+\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert)^{-1}\,dr

\displaystyle\leq Ca_{\varepsilon}(1+\lvert{\log\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert}\rvert).

∎

Corollary 4.23
.

Let
z\in\rho(H)
,
x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
,
\zeta\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
be as described at the beginning of Section
4.4.2
,
\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}
be as in (
4.46
),
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{3})
with
\delta_{3}
chosen as in Lemma
4.22
,
a_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{1/6}
,
\widetilde{\varepsilon}\in(-2\varepsilon_{2},2\varepsilon_{2})\setminus\{0\}
, and
Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}
be as in (
4.29
). Then,

\displaystyle\big\|

\displaystyle\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big(d^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}

\displaystyle-d_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})\big)\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\leq Ca_{\varepsilon}(1+\lvert{\log\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert}\rvert),

where
C
does not depend on
\varepsilon
,
\widetilde{\varepsilon}
, and
x_{0}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Lemma
4.22
and
Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}\in C^{1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N\times N})
yield

\displaystyle\big\|

\displaystyle\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big(d^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}

\displaystyle-d_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})\big)\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}d^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)\big(Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}-Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})\big)\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle+\big\|\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big(d^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)

\displaystyle-d_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)\big)\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigl(\|d^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}a_{\varepsilon}+a_{\varepsilon}(1+\lvert{\log\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert}\rvert)\bigr),

where
C
does not depend on
\varepsilon
,
\widetilde{\varepsilon}
, and
x_{0}^{\prime}
. Moreover, (
4.28
), (
4.50
), and
\zeta\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
imply for the integral kernel
k
of
d_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)

|k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})|\leq C(|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{1-\theta}e^{-c|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|},\quad x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1},

where
c=\tfrac{C_{6}}{C_{4}}>0
with
C_{4}
from (
4.45
) and
C_{6}
from (
4.50
),
and therefore Lemma
A.1
implies

\displaystyle\|d^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}}(|z^{\prime}|+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{1-\theta}e^{-c|z^{\prime}|}\,dz^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}(r+|\widetilde{\varepsilon}|)^{1-\theta}e^{-cr}r^{\theta-2}\,dr

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\lvert{\log\lvert{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\rvert}\rvert),

which completes the proof.
∎

Now, we transfer in Proposition
4.24
the results regarding
e_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(z)
and
d^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}(z)
to
E_{\varepsilon}(z)
and
D^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\varepsilon}(z)
. The first, second, and third estimate in Proposition
4.24
are consequences of Lemma
4.20
, Lemma
4.21
, and Corollary
4.23
, respectively.
Recall that
\zeta\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
is described in the beginning of Section
4.4.2
,
\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}
is defined by (
4.46
), and
\delta_{3}
is chosen as in Lemma
4.22
.

Proposition 4.24
.

Let
z\in\rho(H)
,
x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{3})
,
a_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{1/6}
,
Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}
be as in (
4.29
), and
\psi\in C^{1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
. Then, the operators
E_{\varepsilon}(z)
and
[D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z),\psi]
act as bounded operators from
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
to
\mathcal{B}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
and

\displaystyle\|E_{\varepsilon}(z)\|_{0\to 1}\leq C\frac{1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert}{a_{\varepsilon}},

\displaystyle\|[D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\psi]\|_{0\to 1}\leq C{\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}(1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert),

\displaystyle\big\|\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big(D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}-D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})\big)\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big\|_{0\to 0}\leq Ca_{\varepsilon}(1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert),

where
C>0
does not depend on
x_{0}^{\prime}
and
\varepsilon
.

Proof.

We start by showing that
[D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\psi]
is well-defined as an operator from
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
to
\mathcal{B}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
. Let
f\in\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
and set
g=[D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\psi]f
. It follows from (
4.27
) that
g
has for
t\in(-1,1)
the representation

g(t)=\int_{-1}^{1}[d_{\varepsilon(t-s)}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\psi]f(s)\,ds.

(4.57)

Since
[d_{\varepsilon(t-s)}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\psi]
has the continuity property from Lemma
4.21
and
f\in\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
,
[
20
, Proposition 1.1.28]
implies that the function

(-1,1)\times(-1,1)\ni(t,s)\mapsto[d_{\varepsilon(t-s)}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\psi]f(s)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})

is measurable. According to Lemma
4.21
we have

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\bigg(\int_{-1}^{1}\|[d_{\varepsilon(t-s)}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\psi]f(s)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\,ds\bigg)^{2}\,dt

(4.58)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\psi\|_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\bigg(\int_{-1}^{1}(1+\lvert{\log\lvert{\varepsilon(t-s)}\rvert}\rvert)\|f(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\,ds\bigg)^{2}\,dt.

This expression can be estimated with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Fubini’s theorem by

\displaystyle C{\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\biggl(\int_{-1}^{1}(1+\lvert{\log\lvert{\varepsilon(t-s)}\rvert}\rvert)\,ds

(4.59)

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{-1}^{1}(1+\lvert{\log\lvert{\varepsilon(t-s)}\rvert}\rvert){\lVert{f(s)}\rVert}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}^{2}\,ds\biggr)\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C{\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}^{2}\bigg(\int_{-2}^{2}(1+\lvert{\log\lvert{\varepsilon s}\rvert}\rvert)\,ds\bigg)^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}{\lVert{f(s)}\rVert}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C\big({\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}(1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert){\lVert{f}\rVert}_{0}\big)^{2}.

In particular, applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality again gives us

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}

\displaystyle\|[d_{\varepsilon(t-s)}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\psi]f(s)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\int_{-1}^{1}\bigg(\int_{-1}^{1}\|[d_{\varepsilon(t-s)}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\psi]f(s)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\,ds\bigg)^{2}\,dt}<\infty.

Thus, Fubini’s theorem for Bochner integrals, see
[
20
, Proposition 1.2.7]
, shows that
g(t)
in (
4.57
) is well-defined and measurable as a function from
(-1,1)
to
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
. Moreover, (
4.57
), (
4.58
), and (
4.59
) also give us the norm estimate as

\displaystyle\|[D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\psi]f\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle={\lVert{g}\rVert}_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{-1}^{1}\|g(t)\|^{2}_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-1}^{1}\bigg(\int_{-1}^{1}\|[d_{\varepsilon(t-s)}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\psi]f(s)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\,ds\bigg)^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\big({\lVert{\psi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}(1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert){\lVert{f}\rVert}_{0}\big)^{2}.

The proof of the assertion regarding
E_{\varepsilon}(z)
follows along the same lines if one applies Lemma
4.20
instead of Lemma
4.21
. Moreover, since

\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big(D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}-D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})\big)\chi_{B(x_{0}^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}

is only considered as an operator in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
,
one only has to prove the norm estimate in this topology. The norm can be estimated in the same way as we estimated the norm of
E_{\varepsilon}(z)
by applying Corollary
4.23
instead of Lemma
4.21
.
∎

As the last part of our local analysis we state a result concerning the inverse of
I+D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})q
for
x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
. This is an important result as these operators are going to play an essential role when constructing the inverse of
I+D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}q
. Recall that
\zeta\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
and
\Sigma
are as described in the beginning of Section
4.4.2
,
\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}
is given by (
4.46
), and
q
is as in (
1.4
).

Proposition 4.25
.

Let
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
,
\eta,\tau\in C^{1}_{b}(\Sigma;\mathbb{R})
,
d=\eta^{2}-\tau^{2}
such that

\sup_{x_{\Sigma}\in\Sigma}d(x_{\Sigma})<\frac{\pi^{2}}{4},

and
Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}
be as in (
4.29
). Then, there exists a
\delta_{4}>0
such that the operators
(I+D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})q)^{-1}
are uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
with respect to
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{4})
and
x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
.

Proof.

Note that

Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})=\eta(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime}))I_{N}+\tau(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime}))\beta=Q_{\eta(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})),\tau(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime}))}

for
x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
; cf. (
4.29
) and (
4.30
). Moreover, for every
x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
the set
\Sigma_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}
is an affine hyperplane in
\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
and therefore there exists a
y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}
and a
\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})\in\textup{SO}(\theta)
such that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}

\displaystyle=\{\kappa(x^{\prime},\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})):x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})\big(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\times\{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime})\}\big)=\Sigma_{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})}.

Now, let
\overline{B}_{\varepsilon}^{\Sigma_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}}(z)=\overline{B}_{\varepsilon}^{\Sigma_{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})}}(z)
be defined as
\overline{B}_{\varepsilon}(z)
in (
4.18
) with
\Sigma
substituted by
\Sigma_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}=\Sigma_{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})}
. According to Proposition
4.17
the operator
I+D_{\varepsilon}^{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})}(z)Q_{\eta(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})),\tau(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime}))}q
is continuously invertible in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
. Moreover, we get from (
4.22
)

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle+D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})q=\iota_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}\bigl(I+\overline{B}_{\varepsilon}^{\Sigma_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}}(z)Q_{\eta(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})),\tau(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime}))}q\bigr)\iota_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\iota_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}\bigl(I+\overline{B}_{\varepsilon}^{\Sigma_{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})}}(z)Q_{\eta(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})),\tau(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime}))}q\bigr)\iota_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\iota_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}\iota_{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})}^{-1}\bigl(I+D_{\varepsilon}^{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})}(z)Q_{\eta(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})),\tau(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime}))}q\bigr)\iota_{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})}\iota_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}^{-1}

and from (
4.21
)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\lVert{\iota_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}}\rVert}_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}={\lVert{\iota_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}^{-1}}\rVert}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\Sigma_{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa};\mathbb{C}^{N})}^{-1}

\displaystyle=(1+\lvert{\nabla\zeta(x_{0}^{\prime})}\rvert^{2})^{-1/4},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\lVert{\iota_{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})}}\rVert}_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle={\lVert{\iota_{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})}^{-1}}\rVert}_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}^{-1}

\displaystyle=1.

These considerations show that
I+D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})q
is also continuously invertible in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
and

\displaystyle\big\|(I+D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})q)^{-1}\big\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|(I+D_{\varepsilon}^{y_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime}),\widetilde{\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})}(z)Q_{\eta(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime})),\tau(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x_{0}^{\prime}))}q)^{-1}\big\|_{0\to 0}.

Now, the result follows from applying Corollary
4.18
(for
S=\overline{\textup{ran }(\eta,\tau)}
) if one chooses
\delta_{4}=\delta_{2}(\overline{\textup{ran }(\eta,\tau)})>0
, where
\delta_{2}
was introduced in Corollary
4.18
.
∎

Inspired by the local principle in
[
31
, Proposition 5]
, see also
[
29
,
30
]
, we are going to construct partitions of unity which allow us to globalize the established local results. We start by choosing a partition of unity
(\phi_{n^{\prime}})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
for
\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
with uniformly bounded derivatives which satisfies
\textup{supp }\phi_{n^{\prime}}\subset B(n^{\prime},1)
for
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
. Moreover, let
(\vartheta_{n^{\prime}})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
be a sequence of functions with uniformly bounded derivatives which fulfils
0\leq\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}\leq 1
,
\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}=1
on
B(n^{\prime},2)
and
\textup{supp }\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}\subset B(n^{\prime},3)
for
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
. According to Proposition
A.2
such sequences exist.
By defining for
a\in(0,\varepsilon_{2}^{1/6})
and
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
the functions
\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a}(\cdot)=\phi_{n^{\prime}}(\cdot/a)
and
\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}(\cdot)=\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}(\cdot/a)
we obtain similar sequences with scaled supports; in particular
(\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
is a partition of unity for
\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
. Furthermore, there exists a
C>0
which does not depend on
a
such that

\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\max\{\|\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a}\|_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})},\|\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}\|_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}\}<\frac{C}{a}.

(4.60)

Before we can construct the inverse of
I+D_{\varepsilon}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}q
we have to deal with series of operators. Let
(A_{n^{\prime}})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
be a family of bounded operators mapping from a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
to a Hilbert space
\mathcal{G}
.
If the sequence of partial sums
S_{n}=\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1},\lvert{n^{\prime}}\rvert\leq n}A_{n^{\prime}}
converges in the strong sense to an operator, then we define

\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}^{\textup{st.}}A_{n^{\prime}}:=\underset{n\to\infty}{\textup{s-lim}}\,S_{n}.

According to the Banach-Steinhaus theorem,
\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}^{\textup{st.}}A_{n^{\prime}}
is again a bounded operator mapping from
\mathcal{H}
to
\mathcal{G}
.
Moreover, the definition of the series implies that if
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are Hilbert spaces, and
U:\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{H}
and
V:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are bounded operators, then

\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}^{\textup{st.}}VA_{n^{\prime}}U:\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime}

is a well-defined bounded operator and

V\Big(\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}^{\textup{st.}}A_{n^{\prime}}\Big)U=\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}^{\textup{st.}}VA_{n^{\prime}}U

holds.
If
(\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
is a uniformly bounded sequence of operators in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
, then according to Proposition
A.4
the series
\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}^{\textup{st.}}\vartheta^{a}_{n^{\prime}}\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\vartheta^{a}_{n^{\prime}}

converges in the strong sense in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
and we have

\Big\|\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}^{\textup{st.}}\vartheta^{a}_{n^{\prime}}\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\vartheta^{a}_{n^{\prime}}\Big\|_{0\to 0}\leq 11^{\theta-1}\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\|\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\|_{0\to 0}.

(4.61)

Moreover, if
(\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
is also uniformly bounded as a sequence of operators from
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
to
\mathcal{B}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
, then
\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}^{\textup{st.}}\vartheta^{a}_{n^{\prime}}\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\vartheta^{a}_{n^{\prime}}
acts also as a bounded operator from
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
to
\mathcal{B}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
and

\Big\|\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}^{\textup{st.}}\vartheta^{a}_{n^{\prime}}\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\vartheta^{a}_{n^{\prime}}\Big\|_{0\to 1}\leq\frac{C}{a}\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}{\lVert{\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}}\rVert}_{0\to 1}

(4.62)

holds, with
C>0
independent of
a
, see also Proposition
A.4
. Before we use these essential observations in the proof of Proposition
4.27
, we state a helpful preliminary lemma.

Lemma 4.26
.

Let
z\in\rho(H)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{2})
,
a_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{1/6}
,
D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)
be as in (
4.22
), and
E_{\varepsilon}(z)
be as in (
4.48
). Then, for any fixed
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}

(1-\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}})E_{\varepsilon}(z)\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}=(1-\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}})D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}.

Proof.

We prove this by showing that the difference of the integral kernels of the operators
(1-\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}})E_{\varepsilon}(z)\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}
and
(1-\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}})D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}
is zero. By (
4.26
), (
4.47
), and (
4.48
) this difference is given by

\displaystyle(1-\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(x^{\prime}))\bigl(\omega\bigl(\tfrac{x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)-1\bigr)\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(y^{\prime})

(4.63)

\displaystyle\cdot G_{z}(x_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime})-x_{\zeta,\kappa}(y^{\prime})+\varepsilon(t-s)\nu_{\zeta,\kappa}(x^{\prime}))\sqrt{1+\lvert{\nabla\zeta(y^{\prime})}\rvert^{2}}

for all
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
and
t,s\in(-1,1)
. If
y^{\prime}\notin B(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime},a_{\varepsilon})
, then
\tfrac{y^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\notin B(n^{\prime},1)\supset\textup{supp }\phi_{n^{\prime}}
and therefore

\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(y^{\prime})=\phi_{n^{\prime}}\bigl(\tfrac{y^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)=0.

Furthermore, if
x^{\prime}\in B(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime},2a_{\varepsilon})
, then
\tfrac{x^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\in B(n^{\prime},2)
and hence as
\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}=1
on
B(n^{\prime},2)
we have

1-\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(x^{\prime})=1-\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}\bigl(\tfrac{x^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)=0.

These two observations show that if
x^{\prime}\in B(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime},2a_{\varepsilon})
or
y^{\prime}\notin B(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime},a_{\varepsilon})
, then (
4.63
) vanishes. Thus, it remains to consider the case
x^{\prime}\notin B(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime},2a_{\varepsilon})
and
y^{\prime}\in B(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime},a_{\varepsilon})
. However, this implies
|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|>a_{\varepsilon}
. In this case we use
\omega=1
on
\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\setminus B(0,1)
, see the text above (
4.48
), to obtain

\omega\bigl(\tfrac{x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}}{a_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)-1=0.

This shows that (
4.63
) vanishes for all
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
and
t,s\in(-1,1)
.
∎

Proposition 4.27
.

Let
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
,
\zeta\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})
and
\Sigma
be as described in the beginning of Section
4.4.2
,
\eta,\tau\in C^{1}_{b}(\Sigma;\mathbb{R})
,
d=\eta^{2}-\tau^{2}
satisfy

\sup_{x_{\Sigma}\in\Sigma}d(x_{\Sigma})<\frac{\pi^{2}}{4},

Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}
be as in (
4.29
) and
q
be as in (
1.4
). Then, there exists a
\delta_{5}\in(0,\varepsilon_{2})
, with
\varepsilon_{2}>0
from Proposition
4.6
, such that
I+D^{\zeta,\kappa}_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}q
has a bounded right inverse which is uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
with respect to
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{5})
.

Proof.

The proof is split into four steps. In
Step 1
we define
R_{\varepsilon}
which will turn out to be a first approximation for the right inverse of
I+D^{\zeta,\kappa}_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}q
. Moreover, in this step we also show that
R_{\varepsilon}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
with respect to
\varepsilon
. Then, in
Step 2
we calculate
(I+D^{\zeta,\kappa}_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}q)R_{\varepsilon}
. Afterwards, we find in
Step 3
that
(I+D^{\zeta,\kappa}_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}q)R_{\varepsilon}
equals
I+K_{\varepsilon}+L_{\varepsilon}
, where
K_{\varepsilon}
and
L_{\varepsilon}
fulfil the inequalities

\|K_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 1}\leq C\frac{1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert}{a_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\\
\quad\textup{and}\quad\|L_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 0}\leq Ca_{\varepsilon}(1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert).

(4.64)

Based on these observations we define in
Step 4
an operator
\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon}
which is uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
with respect to
\varepsilon
and fulfils
(I+D^{\zeta,\kappa}_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}q)\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon}=I+\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon}
, where
\|\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 0}
can be estimated by
C(1+|\log(\varepsilon)|)\varepsilon^{1/6-r}
for an
r\in(0,\tfrac{1}{6})
. In particular, this shows that for sufficiently small
\varepsilon>0
the right inverse of
I+D^{\zeta,\kappa}_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}q
is given by the operator
\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon}(I+\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon})^{-1}
and uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
with respect to
\varepsilon
.

Step 1.

We define for
\varepsilon\in(0,\min\{\varepsilon_{2},\delta_{4}\})
, where
\varepsilon_{2}
and
\delta_{4}
are chosen as in Proposition
4.6
and Proposition
4.25
, respectively,

\displaystyle R_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})\to\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}),

\displaystyle R_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:=\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}.

Here,
a_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{1/6}
and
R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}:=(I+D^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}_{\varepsilon}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})q)^{-1}
with
\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}}=\zeta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}
as in (
4.46
) for
x_{0}^{\prime}=a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}
. The equality
\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}=\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}
implies

R_{\varepsilon}=\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}\phi_{n}^{a_{\varepsilon}}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}

and therefore Proposition
4.25
and (
4.61
) show that
R_{\varepsilon}
is well-defined and uniformly bounded by

{\lVert{R_{\varepsilon}}\rVert}_{0\to 0}\leq 11^{\theta-1}\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}{\lVert{(I+D^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}_{\varepsilon}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})q)^{-1}}\rVert}_{0\to 0}\leq C,

(4.65)

where
C>0
does not depend on
\varepsilon
.

Step 2.

Applying
I+D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q
to
R_{\varepsilon}
yields

\displaystyle(I+D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)R_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}(I+D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle=\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(I+D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle+\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}(1-\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}})D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}.

Moreover, using Lemma
4.26
gives us

\displaystyle(I+

\displaystyle D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)R_{\varepsilon}=\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(I+D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle+\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}(1-\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}})E_{\varepsilon}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle=\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(I+D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q-E_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle+E_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}qR_{\varepsilon}.

Writing
D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}
as

\displaystyle D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})q\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}+\big(D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}-D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})\big)q\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle=\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})q+[D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}]Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})q

\displaystyle+\big(D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}-D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})\big)q\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}\

and introducing the operators
L_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}:=\big(D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}-D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})\big)q\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}
and
K_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}:=[D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}]Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})q-E_{\varepsilon}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}
yields

\displaystyle(I+D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)

\displaystyle Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)R_{\varepsilon}

(4.66)

\displaystyle=\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(I+D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})q)R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle+\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(K_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}+L_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon})R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}+E_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}qR_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=I+\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}(K_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}+L_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon})R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}+E_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}qR_{\varepsilon},

where

(I+D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})q)R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}=I\quad\textup{and}\quad\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}=\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}=1

were used.

Step 3.

We start this step by setting

K_{\varepsilon}:=\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}K_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}+E_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}qR_{\varepsilon}

and

L_{\varepsilon}:=\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}L_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}.

Then, (
4.66
) shows

(I+D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}q)R_{\varepsilon}=I+K_{\varepsilon}+L_{\varepsilon}.

(4.67)

Since
R_{\varepsilon}
and
D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)
are uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
, see
Step 1
and the text above (
4.23
), respectively, this implies that also
K_{\varepsilon}+L_{\varepsilon}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
. Moreover,
Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}\in C^{1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N\times N})
, Proposition
4.24
, and (
4.60
) imply

\displaystyle{\lVert{K_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}}\rVert}_{0\to 1}

\displaystyle\leq{\lVert{[D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}]Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})q}\rVert}_{0\to 1}+{\lVert{E_{\varepsilon}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}}\rVert}_{0\to 1}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigl({\lVert{[D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z),\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}]}\rVert}_{0\to 1}+{\lVert{E_{\varepsilon}(z)}\rVert}_{0\to 1}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigl({\lVert{\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}(1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert)+\frac{1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert}{a_{\varepsilon}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert}{a_{\varepsilon}},\qquad n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}.

In turn, with (
4.62
), (
4.65
),
Q_{\eta,\tau}^{\zeta,\kappa}\in C^{1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N\times N})
, Proposition
4.24
, and Proposition
4.25
we get

\displaystyle{\lVert{K_{\varepsilon}}\rVert}_{0\to 1}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl\|\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}K_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}\Bigr\|_{0\to 1}+\|E_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}qR_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 1}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\frac{C}{a_{\varepsilon}}\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\|K_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 1}+\|E_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}qR_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 1}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\frac{C}{a_{\varepsilon}}\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\|K_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 1}+\|E_{{\varepsilon}}(z)\|_{0\to 1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigl(\frac{1}{a_{\varepsilon}}\cdot\frac{1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert}{a_{\varepsilon}}+\frac{1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert}{a_{\varepsilon}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert}{a_{\varepsilon}^{2}}.

Similar we estimate
L_{\varepsilon}
with (
4.61
), Proposition
4.25
, and Proposition
4.24
by

\displaystyle\|L_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}L_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}\Big\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\sum^{\textup{st.}}_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}\chi_{B(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}L_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}R_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}\Big\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\|\chi_{B(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}L_{n^{\prime},\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle=C\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\|\chi_{B(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\big(D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}-D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})\big)q\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle=C\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\|\chi_{B(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\cdot\big(D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}-D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta_{a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime}},\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime})\big)q\chi_{B(a_{\varepsilon}n^{\prime},3a_{\varepsilon})}\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{a_{\varepsilon}}\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle\leq Ca_{\varepsilon}(1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert).

This shows that (
4.64
) is valid and hence completes
Step 3
.

Step 4.

We note that Proposition
4.9
, (
4.20
), (
4.22
) and (
4.29
) imply that
I+D_{0}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q
is continuously invertible in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
and
\mathcal{B}^{1/2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
.
Furthermore, since
R_{\varepsilon}
and
K_{\varepsilon}+L_{\varepsilon}
are uniformly bounded operators in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
, the operator

\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon}:=R_{\varepsilon}-(I+D_{0}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}(K_{\varepsilon}+L_{\varepsilon})

is also uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
. Moreover,
applying
I+D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q
to
\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon}
and (
4.67
) give us

\displaystyle(I

\displaystyle+D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon}

(4.68)

\displaystyle=I+K_{\varepsilon}+L_{\varepsilon}-(I+D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)(I+D_{0}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}(K_{\varepsilon}+L_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=I+(D_{0}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)-D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z))Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q(I+D_{0}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}(K_{\varepsilon}+L_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=I+\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon}

with

\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon}:=(D_{0}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)-D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z))Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q(I+D_{0}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}(K_{\varepsilon}+L_{\varepsilon}).

Thus, by using the estimates for
L_{\varepsilon}
and
K_{\varepsilon}
from
Step 3
and (
4.23
) for a fixed
r\in(0,\tfrac{1}{6})
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle\leq\|(D_{{0}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)-D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z))Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q(I+D_{{0}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}K_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle+\|(D_{{0}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)-D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z))Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q(I+D_{{0}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}L_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle\leq\|(D_{{0}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)-D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z))Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q\|_{1/2\to 0}

\displaystyle\cdot\|(I+D_{{0}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}\|_{1/2\to 1/2}\|K_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 1/2}

\displaystyle+\|(D_{{0}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)-D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z))Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle\cdot\|(I+D_{{0}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}\|_{0\to 0}\|L_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle\leq\|(D_{{0}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)-D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z))Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q\|_{1/2\to 0}

\displaystyle\cdot\|(I+D_{{0}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}\|_{1/2\to 1/2}\|K_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 1}

\displaystyle+\|(D_{{0}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)-D_{\varepsilon}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z))Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle\cdot\|(I+D_{{0}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q)^{-1}\|_{0\to 0}\|L_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon^{1/2-r}(1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert)}{a_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+a_{\varepsilon}(1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert)\bigg)

\displaystyle=C(1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert)(\varepsilon^{1/6-r}+\varepsilon^{1/6})

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\lvert{\log(\varepsilon)}\rvert)\varepsilon^{1/6-r}.

This shows that if we choose
\delta_{5}>0
sufficiently small, then
\|\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon}\|_{0\to 0}<\tfrac{1}{2}
for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{5})
and
(I+\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon})^{-1}
is uniformly bounded with respect to
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{5})
. Thus, as
\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon}
is also uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
,
\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon}(I+\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon})^{-1}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
with respect to
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{5})
and by (
4.68
) it is also the right inverse of
I+D_{{\varepsilon}}^{\zeta,\kappa}(z)Q^{\zeta,\kappa}_{\eta,\tau}q
.
∎

Proposition 4.28
.

Let
\Sigma
be a rotated graph as described in the beginning of Section
4.4.2
,
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
,
q
be as in (
1.4
),
V=\eta I_{N}+\tau\beta
with
\eta,\tau\in C^{1}_{b}(\Sigma;\mathbb{R})
, and
d=\eta^{2}-\tau^{2}
such that

\sup_{x_{\Sigma}\in\Sigma}d(x_{\Sigma})<\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}.

Then, there exists
\varepsilon_{3}\in(0,\varepsilon_{2})
, with
\varepsilon_{2}>0
from Proposition
4.6
, such that
I+B_{\varepsilon}(z)Vq
has a bounded inverse which is uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\Sigma)
with respect to
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{3})
.

Proof.

We directly get from Proposition
4.27
, (
4.21
), (
4.22
), and (
4.29
) that
I+\overline{B}_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Vq
has a right inverse which is uniformly bounded with respect to
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{5})
with
\delta_{5}
from the previous proposition. Using (
4.19
) shows that then
I+B_{{\varepsilon}}(z)Vq
has a right inverse which is uniformly bounded for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{3})
if
\varepsilon_{3}>0
is chosen small enough. We denote this right inverse
\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(z)
. Then, the right inverse of
I+VqB_{{\varepsilon}}(\overline{z})
is given by
I-Vq\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(\overline{z})B_{\varepsilon}(\overline{z})
. Hence,
(I+VqB_{{\varepsilon}}(\overline{z}))^{*}=I+(B_{{\varepsilon}}(\overline{z}))^{*}Vq
has the uniformly bounded left-inverse
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}(z):=I-(Vq\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(\overline{z})B_{\varepsilon}(\overline{z}))^{*}
. Moreover, by
[
8
, Remark 3.11]
the estimate

\big\|B_{\varepsilon}(z)-(B_{\varepsilon}(\overline{z}))^{*}\big\|_{0\to 0}\leq C\varepsilon,\quad\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{2})

holds.
Thus,

\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}(z)(I+B_{\varepsilon}(z)Vq)=I+\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}(z)(B_{\varepsilon}(z)-(B_{\varepsilon}(\overline{z}))^{*})Vq

(4.69)

converges in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
for
\varepsilon\to 0
to the identity operator
I
. In particular, if
\varepsilon_{3}>0
is chosen small enough, then the right hand side of (
4.69
) is invertible in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{3})
, showing that
I+B_{\varepsilon}(z)Vq
is invertible in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
, i.e.

\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(z)=(I+B_{\varepsilon}(z)Vq)^{-1}.

This concludes the proof since we already know that
\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(z)
is uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
.
∎

Appendix A
Additional results for Section
4.4.2

In this section we provide results which are used in Section
4.4.2
. We begin by stating a convenient version of the Schur test.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
k
be a measurable function in
\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\times\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
with values in
\mathbb{C}^{N\times N}
and
\widetilde{k}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
such that

\lvert{k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})}\rvert\leq\widetilde{k}(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime})\quad\textup{for a.e. }x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}.

Then, the operator
K:L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
acting as

Kf(x^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}}k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})f(y^{\prime})dy^{\prime}

is well-defined and bounded and
\|K\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\leq\|\widetilde{k}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}
. Moreover, if additionally
k\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\times\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N\times N})
and

\sum_{l=1}^{\theta-1}\Bigl|\frac{d}{dx^{\prime}_{l}}k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\Bigr|\leq\widetilde{k}(x^{\prime}-y^{\prime})\quad\textup{for a.e. }x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1},

then
K
also acts as bounded operator from
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
to
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
and
\|K\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})\to H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\leq C\|\widetilde{k}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}
.

Proof.

The first assertion is an immediate consequence of the Schur test, see for instance
[
21
, Chapter III, Example 2.4]
. Next, let us prove the second assertion. We start by choosing
g\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
. Since
k\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}\times\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N\times N})
and
g
is compactly supported, dominated convergence shows that
Kg
is differentiable and

\partial_{l}(Kg)(x^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}}\frac{d}{dx^{\prime}_{l}}k(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})g(y^{\prime})\,dy^{\prime}.

Hence, applying the Schur test shows

{\lVert{Kg}\rVert}_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}\leq C\|\widetilde{k}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}{\lVert{g}\rVert}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

The rest follows from the fact that
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
is dense in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
, the completeness of
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
, and the continuity of
K
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{C}^{N})
.
∎

In the upcoming proposition we construct a partition of unity for
\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
such that the functions have uniformly bounded derivatives.

Proposition A.2
.

There exists a partition of unity
(\phi_{n^{\prime}})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
for
\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
subordinate to
(B(n^{\prime},1))_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
such that
\max_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}{\lVert{\phi_{n^{\prime}}}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}<\infty
. Moreover, there exists a sequence of functions
(\vartheta_{n^{\prime}})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
with
\textup{supp }\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}\subset B(n^{\prime},3)
,
0\leq\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}\leq 1
,
\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}=1
on
B(n^{\prime},2)
for all
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
and
\max_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}{\lVert{\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}<\infty
.

Proof.

Note that since
\theta\in\{2,3\}
, the family
