(B(n^{\prime},3/4))_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
is also an open cover of
\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
. We start by choosing a function
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
such that
0\leq\phi\leq 1
,
\phi=1
on
B(0,3/4)
and
\textup{supp }\phi\subset B(0,1)
. Furthermore, we set
{\widetilde{\phi}}_{n^{\prime}}:=\phi(\cdot-n^{\prime})
for
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
. Then,
0\leq{\widetilde{\phi}}_{n^{\prime}}\leq 1
,
{\widetilde{\phi}}_{n^{\prime}}=1
on
B(n^{\prime},3/4)
and
\textup{supp }{\widetilde{\phi}}_{n^{\prime}}\subset B(n^{\prime},1)
. Next, we fix a bijection
\mathcal{Z}:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
and set
\phi_{\mathcal{Z}(1)}:=\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{Z}(1)}
and

\phi_{\mathcal{Z}(j)}=(1-\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{Z}(1)})\cdot\dots\cdot(1-\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{Z}(j-1)})\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{Z}(j)},\quad j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{1\}.

Then,
\textup{supp }\phi_{n^{\prime}}\subset\textup{supp }\widetilde{\phi}_{n^{\prime}}
,
0\leq\phi_{n^{\prime}}\leq 1
for
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
and on gets via induction for
j\in\mathbb{N}

\sum_{k=1}^{j}\phi_{\mathcal{Z}(k)}=1-\prod_{k=1}^{j}(1-\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{Z}(k)}).

This implies
\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\phi_{n^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\phi_{\mathcal{Z}(j)}(x^{\prime})=1
for
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
. Furthermore, let
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
l\in\{1,\dots,\theta-1\}
, and
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
. We estimate

\displaystyle|\partial_{l}\phi_{\mathcal{Z}(j)}(x^{\prime})|=

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{l}(\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{Z}(j)}(x^{\prime}))\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}(1-\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{Z}(k)}(x^{\prime}))

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{Z}(j)}(x^{\prime})(\partial_{l}\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{Z}(k)}(x^{\prime}))\prod_{r=1,r\neq k}^{j-1}(1-\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{Z}(r)}(x^{\prime}))\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{j}\big|\partial_{l}\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{Z}(k)}(x^{\prime})\big|=\sum_{k=1,x^{\prime}\in B(\mathcal{Z}(k),1)}^{j}\big|\partial_{l}\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{Z}(k)}(x^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{\theta-1}{\lVert{\partial_{l}\phi}\rVert}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})},

where we used that
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1}
can be in at most
2^{\theta-1}
balls of the form
B(n^{\prime},1)
with
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
. This shows that the derivatives of the
\phi_{n^{\prime}}
’s are uniformly bounded by
2^{\theta-1}{\lVert{\phi}\rVert}_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1};\mathbb{R})}
. Next we construct the sequence
(\vartheta_{n^{\prime}})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
. To do so, we choose
\vartheta\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
such that
0\leq\vartheta\leq 1
,
\theta=1
on
B(0,2)
and
\textup{supp }\vartheta\subset B(0,3)
. Then, we define
\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}:=\vartheta(\cdot-n^{\prime})
. The constructed sequence has the claimed properties.
∎

Our next goal is to use the functions
\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}
,
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
, from Proposition
A.2
to construct operators based on a uniformly bounded sequence of operators. We start by providing an useful variant of the Cotlar-Stein lemma.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{G}
be Hilbert spaces, let
(\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
be a family of uniformly bounded operators acting from
\mathcal{H}
to
\mathcal{G}
. Moreover, assume that there exists a number
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
exist at most
N
indices
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
such
\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{A}_{m^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\mathcal{A}_{m^{\prime}}^{*}
are nonzero operators. Then, the sum
\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1},|n^{\prime}|<n}\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}
converges for
n\to\infty
in the strong sense to a bounded operator
\mathcal{A}
(which is also denoted by
\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}^{\textup{st.}}\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}
). Moreover, its norm can be estimated by

\|\mathcal{A}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{G}}\leq N\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\|\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{G}}.

Proof.

Our assumptions guarantee

\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\|\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\mathcal{A}_{m^{\prime}}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}}^{1/2}\leq N\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\|\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{G}}

and

\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\|\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{A}_{m^{\prime}}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{1/2}\leq N\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}\|\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{G}}.

Hence, the assertions follow form the Cotlar-Stein lemma, see
[
16
, Lemma 18.6.5]
.
∎

Proposition A.4
.

Let
a\in(0,b)
for a
b>0
,
(\vartheta_{n^{\prime}})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
be the sequence from Proposition
A.2
,
\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}:=\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}\bigl(\tfrac{\cdot}{a}\bigr)
for
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
, and
(A_{n^{\prime}})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
be a sequence of uniformly bounded operators in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
. Then,

A=\sum_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}^{\textup{st.}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}A_{n^{\prime}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}

is a well-defined operator in
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
with

\|A\|_{0\rightarrow 0}\leq 11^{\theta-1}\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}{\lVert{A_{n^{\prime}}}\rVert}_{0\to 0}
.
Moreover, if
(A_{n^{\prime}})_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}
is also a family of uniformly bounded operators acting from
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
to
\mathcal{B}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
, then
A
acts also as a bounded operator from
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
to
\mathcal{B}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
and
{\lVert{A}\rVert}_{0\to 1}\leq\tfrac{C}{a}\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}{\lVert{A_{n^{\prime}}}\rVert}_{0\to 1}
, where
C>0
does not depend on
a\in(0,b)
.

Proof.

Let us start by proving the assertion where we consider
A
and
A_{n^{\prime}}
,
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
, as operators acting from
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
to
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
. We set
\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}:=\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}A_{n^{\prime}}\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}
. Since a fixed ball
B(an^{\prime},3a)
overlaps with at most
11^{\theta-1}
balls of the type
B(am^{\prime},3a)
,
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
, there exist for every
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
at most
N=11^{\theta-1}
indices
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
such that
\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\mathcal{A}_{m^{\prime}}^{*}\neq 0
and
\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{A}_{m^{\prime}}\neq 0
. Moreover, we have for all
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}

\|\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\|_{0\to 0}\leq\|\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}\|_{0\to 0}\|A_{n^{\prime}}\|_{0\to 0}\|\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}\|_{0\to 0}\leq\|A_{n^{\prime}}\|_{0\to 0}.

Thus, by Lemma
A.3
we conclude
\|A\|_{0\rightarrow 0}\leq 11^{\theta-1}\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}{\lVert{A_{n^{\prime}}}\rVert}_{0\to 0}
.

Next, we assume that
A_{n^{\prime}}
,
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1},
act as uniformly bounded operators from
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
to
\mathcal{B}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
. Using again the fact that a fixed ball
B(an^{\prime},3a)
overlaps with at most
11^{\theta-1}
balls of the type
B(am^{\prime},3a)
,
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
, shows that there exist for every
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
at most
N=11^{\theta-1}
indices
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
such that
\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\mathcal{A}_{m^{\prime}}^{0*1}\neq 0
and
\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}^{0*1}\mathcal{A}_{m^{\prime}}\neq 0
, where the expressions
{\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}}^{0*1}
and
{\mathcal{A}_{m^{\prime}}}^{0*1}
denote the adjoint operators of
\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{m^{\prime}}
, respectively, considered as operators mapping from
\mathcal{B}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
to
\mathcal{B}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})
. Furthermore, for all
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}
the inequality

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{n^{\prime}}\|_{0\to 1}

\displaystyle\leq\|\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}\|_{1\to 1}\|A_{n^{\prime}}\|_{0\to 1}\|\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}\|_{0\to 0}

\displaystyle\leq\|\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}\|_{1\to 1}\|A_{n^{\prime}}\|_{0\to 1}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}^{a}\|_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}\|A_{n^{\prime}}\|_{0\to 1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{a}\|\vartheta_{n^{\prime}}\|_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\theta-1})}\|A_{n^{\prime}}\|_{0\to 1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{a}\|A_{n^{\prime}}\|_{0\to 1}

is valid.
Thus, Lemma
A.3
yields
{\lVert{A}\rVert}_{0\to 1}\leq\tfrac{C}{a}\sup_{n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta-1}}{\lVert{A_{n^{\prime}}}\rVert}_{0\to 1}
.
∎
